Fold and observe

function fold(N)    { return (257 - N % 257) % 257; }   // negation; fixed point 0
function observe(N) { return (N + 1) % 257; }            // the unit operator
// fold(observe(N)) = 256 - N ; fixed point 128 ; (fold∘observe)² = identity

OBSERVE prints all four values for a position and, for a CESD seed, the returning seed under fold∘observe with a round-trip check. The gap fold(N) − fold(observe(N)) is always 1, and observe∘fold − fold∘observe is always 2.

The fold operator is a separate stateful thing on the bridge object. FOLD arms it with seed = the NanO₂ public key if an identity is active, else the PC. It computes the partner 257 − seed, the cure partner KA = 12 × seed, and begins a walk whose polarity is DISC for steps 0–127 and ANTI for 128–255, flashing the FOLD LED at every 128th step, "83¹²⁸ ≡ −1, the fold is the heartbeat." If hardware is connected it sends fold_init and then fold_step on every tick. FOLD again disarms; FOLD STATUS reports the walk.

Instrument · terminal

Terminal commands

Six keydown listeners share the single input line. The base handler, registered first, is a bubble-phase listener. Five later handlers (hardware bridge, settlement, mesh and analysis, model bridge, NPU) are capture-phase listeners, and on the target element capture listeners run before bubble listeners, so every add-on handler gets first refusal and returns after stopImmediatePropagation. Among the add-ons, registration order decides. Three consequences are visible from the keyboard:

  • TOWER alone reaches the Fermat layer tower in the analysis handler; the base handler's Galois tower answers only TOWER k with a number.
  • TAPE alone reaches the hardware-bridge handler's tape-structure readout for the current position; the phonograph answers every TAPE <sub> form.
  • HANDSHAKE with a network name and a hex key goes to settlement; HANDSHAKE 72 with a field value goes to NanO₂. SEND text with hardware connected and an identity active is signed on the device; otherwise it is the NanO₂ send.

Anything unmatched is treated as a word lookup: exact match first, then substring, and the machine navigates there. A bare number 0–256 navigates directly and prints the position's dlog, inverse and fold.

Instrument · identity

NanO₂ messaging

A Diffie–Hellman scheme inside GF(257). ID name picks a random secret s in 1–255 and publishes 3ˢ. HANDSHAKE p with a peer's public value sets the shared secret pˢ. Text is encoded word by word: a dictionary word from position 27 upward becomes its position, anything else becomes letters A–Z as 1–26, and words are separated by 0. Encryption multiplies every nonzero position by the shared secret mod 257; decryption multiplies by its inverse. The checksum is the product of the positions mod 210. The wire format is

N2:P<public>:<p.p.p…>:CS<checksum>

The BRIAN identity in the key file uses public key 136 (VIRGIN MARY) and peer 72 (BITCOIN). Its secret, shared secret and decrypt value are in the private key file and deliberately absent from this document. A stored BLE identity named BrianKeys, captured 2026-08-31, carries the same public parameters and is what the PDF reads as a program.

Instrument · hardware

Hardware bridge

CONNECT tries three transports in order. First it polls the entity server at 172.18.0.15:8258/field (or /csi/field behind the proxy) and, if the entity is measuring, goes live in CSI mode with a two-second poll. Failing that it asks for a WebSerial port at 115200 baud. CONNECT ip opens a WebSocket to port 8257. Serial and WebSocket speak the same newline-delimited JSON, one pending reply at a time with a five-second timeout.

The entity endpoint

The CSI pane polls /field every five seconds and renders a FIELD card and four PICO cards labelled by eigenaxis φ², −1, −1, ψ². Fields consumed: node, word, ray, depth, dlog, fold and fold_word, inv and inv_word, observer_flip and flip_word, drive and drive_word, pit and pit_frac, qr, cf_r, psi_err, gamma, ticks, uptime, devices, measured, rssi, and picos A–D each with rays, energy and lead. /devices lists decomposed BLE devices with mac, rssi, count and word. RSSI is mapped to an impedance shell and fed to the vertex fill of the interpreter.

Firmware structure as the FIRMWARE command describes it: a C11 tree with a 257-cell VM core, SHA-3, a Pisot hasher with φ and ψ channels, Ed25519, a ledger with Cassini decay, a node with peers and pending transactions, and a JSON serial dispatcher; genesis supply 210, block reward φ⁵ × |ψ|ⁱⁿᵈᵉˣ, wire messages TX, BLOCK, GETCHAIN, CHAINTIP, PING, PONG, NVS config with target 172.18.0.15:5005.

Instrument · radio

BLE, serial, RF

  • Web Bluetooth. SCAN BLE opens the device picker and decodes the chosen name's bytes to words; SCAN BLE ALL uses passive scanning for ten seconds where the browser allows it. Every device becomes a mesh peer named BT-<name>. The ESP32-C6 GATT service is c5110001-0257-0003-8000-00805f9b34fb with characteristics …0002 query, …0003 mirror, …0004 devices, …0005 scan, …0006 command.
  • Serial protocols. MIRROR uses single-letter frames prefixed with $ (F, G, D, T, M, R, E, C, X, U, I); TUNNEL uses $Q OPEN, DATA and CLOSE with !Q ACK, NACK, DATA and ECHO replies. The USB path enumerates WebUSB and WebHID devices and counts bytes into the field energy accumulator by position and ray.
  • RF scan. The RF button decomposes access-point MAC bytes into positions with pos = (b + 1) mod 257, while the TUNNEL command and the PDF use b mod 257 and the protocol page reduces a whole address by alternating sum. Three mappings for the same input.
  • Bus export. EXPORT writes a text analysis with a live-device section: serial bytes and frames, USB reads, BLE devices with RSSI ranges and manufacturer data, the field energy per position and the ray accumulator.
Instrument · live readout · 2026-09-04

The other side of the serial

The mirror is a two-way thing. Outbound, the ⇄ MIRROR button writes the browser's field state to the device every 200 ms: the sixteen most energetic positions as $F, the same as glyphs as $G, each one's doubling and halving as $D, its tunnel chain as $T, the single ×55 tunnel multiplier as $M, and the twelve ray energies as $R. Inbound, the device answers JSON commands, the ↓ DUMP button sends the full set, and the BLE path reads the device's own mirror from the Cascade GATT characteristic ending 0003 as a list of positions with energies. On the evening of 2026-09-04 the inbound side was read directly, with the machine page closed so that the port was free.

The node was an ESP32-C6, Espressif USB JTAG/serial debug unit, vendor 0x303A product 0x1001, serial number 58:E6:C5:11:F4:50, on /dev/cu.usbmodem101 at 115200 baud, the rate the machine opens. Chrome held the port until it was closed. The port was then opened with DTR and RTS both asserted, the one state the USB-Serial-JTAG peripheral does not read as a reset, and closed by dropping RTS before DTR; the node did not restart. Its first unsolicited bytes were the tail of a $M frame at tick 44732, so MIRROR had been live from the machine until the moment Chrome closed. The firmware calls itself bwave: it logs CSI frames on channel 4, forwards to a display over uart_cyd, stores tunnel positions per device seen, and runs an accelerometer fall detector, which fired twice during the read.

The field seen from the far end

The cascade replies compute a position on the device. Every dlog, ray, residue class, negation, chart fold and inverse agreed with the tablet: 83 with dlog 15, fold 173, neg 174, inv 96; 5 with dlog 55, fold 251, neg 252, inv 103; 252 with dlog 183, fold 4, neg 5, inv 154. The device therefore uses both folds, and names them: fold is 256 − n and neg is 257 − n. Its five extra fields are the framework's own quantities, and three of them were identified exactly from the three samples, a fourth as a ratio, and one not at all:

at a = 5, 83, 252 trace = 2a 10, 166, 247 impedance = a · 377 ≡ a · 120 86, 194, 171 Z = a · Z₀, with Z₀ = 377 Ω = F(14) ≡ 120 GOLDEN RATIO reflection = (a − 377) · (a + 377)⁻¹ 143, 34, 133 Γ = (Z − Z₀)/(Z + Z₀), the genome's reflection coefficient, the position as Z quality = a · (256 − a)⁻¹ 42, 204, 63 a position over its chart-fold partner norm = ? 174, 113, 234 fits −2a² − 6a − 3 on the three samples; any three points fit a quadratic

The device's pi field gave 9, 2 and 5 at positions 5, 83 and 252. Those are the digits of π at those indices counted from the leading 3, so the firmware carries the correct π where the machine's embedded p column, item 20 of the register, reads 6 at 83. Its word table is a third edition, distinct from both the JSON tablet and the machine's dictionary: 83 is BISMUTH, which is the element whose atomic number is 83 and the word added to the tablet at 83 earlier the same day; 5 is HAMSA, 173 HAFNIUM, 154 YTTERBIUM, 251 HOLY OF HOLIES, 103 PRAYING MANTIS; 4 is BAA and 252 EINSTEIN FIELD EQUATIONS as in the tablet. Also on the wire, not asked for: a CSI table of 32 devices seen, the two busiest at about 35,000 frames each, and a tunnel store that had reached 3,331 devices. Their addresses are not reproduced here.

The FLIP control

The mirror as written was one-way: the $F stream is write-only and nothing in the machine parsed a reply on it, so the other side could only be seen by going around, as the readout above did from a shell. On 2026-09-04 the machine was given the same move as a control. A button labelled ◐ FLIP now sits in the CSI serial row at line 909, between ⇄ MIRROR and ×2, and calls serialBus.flipMirror() at line 15275. It uses only two commands the firmware already answers, so nothing on the device changes and nothing needs to be flashed.

  • With no port open it reports "FLIP: no serial device — connect first" in the status field and stops.
  • If MIRROR is live it pauses the 200 ms outbound frames for the duration of the read, so the reply is not interleaved with the device's echoes, and restarts them in a finally block whatever happens.
  • It sends {"cmd":"flip_status"} and prints the flip state, the x-ray value and the three flip counters; then the NanO₂ block with two checks made in the browser, pub × inv ≡ 1 and fold = 257 − pub, each marked ✓ or ✗; then the device's top positions with word and energy, the device's dlog and ray checked against the machine's own dlog table, a mismatch printing our value beside theirs.
  • It sends {"cmd":"mirror_broadcast"} and prints how many positions the device holds and broadcasts.
  • Everything renders in the main terminal trace in the machine's gold, glow and amber classes, and the parsed result is kept as serialBus._otherSide for other panels. This edition of v4 has no log-out pane, so DUMP in this copy writes only to the console; FLIP does not depend on it.

Against the C6 read that evening the button would print 136, 240, 121 and 72 with every check passing and "48 positions held and broadcast by the device". The whole inline script parses under node after the edit; the button was not pressed against the live device from this side, so its first real press is the author's. The edit added 53 lines and the pre-edit file is kept as a backup with the session's working files.

Instrument · fleet · 2026-09-05

The mining fleet

Five ESP32 boards mine to one wallet on public-pool.io:21496, a solo pool, each under its own worker suffix; the pool's share difficulty is 100 000, so at the fleet's 27 kH/s a share is expected about every 500 years and a block in some 6 × 10¹¹, and nothing is expected back. What the boards do with that time is the part that is ours. Their nonces are not counted up: the base walk takes nonce = chunk × 257 + seed × 3ᵏ, one generator step per hash, and the fold walk covers the 32-bit space as 255 × 255 chunks of 257 × 257 stepped by ×(2 + φ), whose norm is 5, so every fold step is one halving in the norm, with the 131 071 border nonces swept last. The seed and chunk of each base walk come from a SHA-256 of the room's CSI frames. Every board broadcasts a JSON report on UDP 25700 every ten seconds and listens for the others', so each MINER page shows the fleet. The three worker names that are numbers are the counts read off the MI25's VBIOS tape: 6561 = 3⁸ IDENT opcodes, 3125 = 5⁵ JUMP opcodes, 65024 the tape's length; 241 is the count of PRAYER bytes. The source is the C6 mining copy under Documents/cascade_machine, firmware_c6_mine, with the same miner, peer and walk files copied into the two CYD trees; the original firmware tree is untouched.

The identity walk

Each board is an element of the fold. Its WiFi MAC, read at boot as a 48-bit integer n, is written in base 257, a = n mod 257 and b = ⌊n / 257⌋ mod 257, and E = a + bφ is the board. The board finds its own fold dlog k₀, the step at which 2 + φ reaches E, by baby-step giant-step with m = 257: two tables of 257 entries, once at boot, no stored table. It then checks itself: N(2 + φ) = 5 and dlog₃ 5 = 55, so dlog₃ N(E) must equal 55k₀ mod 256, and every board reports that check true. The fold walk opens at E with step k₀ instead of at 1, and at grid cell (a mod 255, b mod 255) instead of (0, 0), so five boards run five rotations of the same 66048-cycle from five cells and never stand on the same step at once. Every chunk is still walked in full, the zero element and 66048 nonzero ones, which also closes an off-by-one in the earlier walk that stopped one short. The low 17 bits of extranonce2 carry a × 257 + b, so a block found by any board would name the board in its coinbase; the bits above are the per-job counter. The reports gained elem, norm, k and the current walk dlog, and the MINER pages an identity line, and each peer row shows the peer's element. A switch to open every board at one fixed cell was added for the third Waveshare at (250, 144), the residues of 2049 and its glyph palindrome, and removed the same evening; it stays in Kconfig, off for the whole fleet.

BoardMACIP tonightWorkerE = a + bφNormk₀RaykH/s

The product of the five elements is 102 + 221φ with norm 39 TEN and fold dlog 7021, the sum of the five k₀ mod 66048, as it must be. The two dies of the MI25 read the same way are 151 + 83φ and 151 + 116φ, 2025 fold steps apart with a ratio of norm 83 PRAYER; the boards and the cards are placed in one space.

The flip in the fleet

The alphabet's mirror does not reach the miners: nothing in the walk is a word. What the fold has in its place is conjugation, a + bφ → (a + b) − bφ, the map x → x²⁵⁷, which keeps the norm and sends fold dlog k to 257k mod 66048. Each board's conjugate is another cell of the same grid: 55 + 63φ pairs with 118 + 194φ at step 39462, 168 + 135φ with 46 + 122φ at 46833, 130 + 65φ with 195 + 192φ at 59855, 127 + 153φ with 23 + 104φ at 51198, and 160 + 246φ with 149 + 11φ at 21897. Every board already passes its conjugate's step inside every chunk, since a chunk covers the whole cycle; the conjugate is a place on the walk, not a second walk. One board touches the new tablet entry directly: the ten-page CYD's norm is 75, SHIFT and now RHENIUM Z, and its own boot check reads 55 × 13449 mod 256 = 111 = dlog₃ 75. The numeric worker names read as positions are 136 VIRGIN MARY, 41 OMEGA, 3 C and 241 TWIN PRIME CONJECTURE, with negations 121 REVELATION, 216 EQUIVALENCE PRINCIPLE, 254 ZERO KNOWLEDGE ROLLUP and 16 BAAL; Atbash has no reading of a numeral. The one place a 75 could be put into the walk is the fixed-cell switch, which is off. None of this changes what a hash is worth, since every nonce is one draw at the same odds, and no firmware was changed for this section.

The ray test and the mark, 2026-09-06

What the field can and cannot see of SHA-256 is exact. 257 divides 2³² − 1, so a 32-bit word reduces to the alternating sum of its bytes and a rotation by k bits is multiplication by 2^(−k mod 16): the ten rotation amounts of Σ₀, Σ₁, σ₀ and σ₁ become the multipliers 193, 8, 253, 253, 32, 128, 255, 193, 129, 225, all in the doubling orbit. 2³² ≡ 1, so addition mod 2³² is field addition minus one per carry out. XOR, the shifts, choose and majority carry no residue rule at all, and every one of the sixty-four rounds passes through them, so nothing of a nonce's field position is expected to reach its hash. Whether anything does is not derivable and was counted two ways. In a parallel session on the Mac, four streams of two million digests each, sixteen million word observations, against the correct null, which is the ray histogram of the position set, 22/256 on rays 0–3 and 21/256 on rays 4–11 and not 1/12, gave χ² of 8.45, 11.06, 7.69 and 19.41 on eleven degrees of freedom, indistinguishable from a uniform control run through the same pipeline; that record is the readout's own section with its hash b87ad2ce…8f0e10 kept beside it. On the boards, every fold-walk hash is now counted against the ray of the step that made it: the hash's residue mod 257 under the target's little-endian reading, the ray of that residue in a 12 × 13 table, and whether it cleared 20 or 24 leading zero bits. The chi-squares are taken from the observed margins, so they do not carry the 1/12 artifact, and their degrees of freedom are 132, 11 and 3072. The tables ride in every UDP report, in the miner and raytest commands, on the walker's /api/raytest, and on each MINER page, behind a Kconfig switch that is on.

The same build writes the boot line's product into the coinbase. extranonce2 now carries the board's element in its low 17 bits, 202 COINBASE TRANSACTION in the eight bits above, and the per-job counter in what the pool's four bytes leave, seven bits; the value in use is in every report as en2. First readings from the three boards flashed that evening, each decoded from its own report:

BoardWorkerextranonce2Identity bitsMarkHashes countedRay × ray χ², df 132Residues χ², df 3072

All three sit where a uniform table sits. Hashes over 20 zero bits arrived at the expected rate, one or two per board in the first minutes, none over 24. Two rates fell against the previous night, the C6 from 7.6 to 6.1 kH/s and the walker from 8.9 to 7.4, while the ten-page held at 4.8; the count costs 32 additions a hash, which does not account for that, and the boards' rates also move with their share of CSI chunks, so the cause is not established and an hour with the switch off against an hour with it on is the measurement. The two Waveshare C6s were not flashed and were not heard on the subnet that day. The pool is solo, so none of this is income: it pays only a full block, to the wallet in the worker name, and at the fleet's rate that is one chance in about a trillion a year; a share is expected in some five hundred years.

The rate test, 2026-09-06 to 07. To measure what the count costs, the first C6 ran one hour on the ray-test image while every board's ten-second report was logged: 3599 s of reports, 22.30 million hashes, exactly half of them CSI-chunk hashes on every board, so the seed ring is saturated and the walks alternate chunk for chunk; 6195 hashes a second overall and 3098 fold hashes a second, reported rate 6.31 kH/s between 6.10 and 6.36. The walker over the same hour: 7376 hashes a second; the ten-page: 4676. An image with the count compiled out and the mark kept was then flashed to the same board at 23:20 UTC, MAC read first, and the second hour never ran: the pool answered a new subscription in about thirty seconds and sent no job after it, while the firmware's subscribe wait is bounded by its ten-second receive timeout, so the board cycled through 92 reconnects, connected and authorised with the pool's difficulty but no work. From the Mac the same pool opened a connection in 0.07 s and answered a subscribe at 30 s with no notify, twice. The two CYDs never dropped their older sessions and were unaffected. The reconnect bound is a firmware limit, reported and not changed: a receive timeout treated as a disconnect during subscribe. The waiter for the board's first report was stopped from the author's side, and the C6 remains on the off image without a job; the hour with the count off has not been measured. During that evening the C6's CSI device list showed ac:eb:e6:1e:1e:28, the .241 Waveshare, transmitting on the WiFi at about two thousand frames while silent on the report port, so that board is associated and still on the fixed-cell image.

What the evening tested

After the identity image went onto the first C6 it could not reach the pool: connect() to 38.51.144.232:21496 failed with errno 113, ECONNABORTED, the SYN never answered, on every attempt, while the board heard its peers and resolved the pool's name. The third Waveshare fell silent in the same minute. Three tests settled where the fault was. The pre-identity image that had logged in at 21 s on the same board an hour before now failed the same way, seven reconnects, so the firmware was cleared. An image pointed at the Mac on the LAN connected, so the board's TCP was cleared. The Mac reached the pool in 0.1 s three times, so the pool and the WAN were cleared. What remained was the router forwarding those two devices, and when it issued new leases every board came back within a minute, the first C6 with zero reconnects. Two things were removed along the way and stay removed: the connect failure is now logged with the resolved address and errno, and the fall detector, which read the second difference of the CSI phase as an acceleration and warned every five seconds, is behind a switch that is off; it was not the cause.

Instrument · value

Settlement and mesh

A compact BigInt secp256k1 with point addition, doubling, scalar multiplication, signing with low-S normalisation, and verification. DERIVE net hashes identity:secret:net:cascade-settlement twice with SHA-256, reduces to a scalar, and maps the public x-coordinate mod 257 to a field position. HANDSHAKE net hex does ECDH against a peer's compressed or uncompressed key, hashes the shared x to an AES-256-GCM key, and reports the shared field position. SEND.net encrypts with a random 12-byte IV, signs the ciphertext, and relays through the hardware bridge if connected and through the mesh if any peer exists; RECV.net and incoming relays decrypt and drop the plaintext's first position into the PC.

Defect, reported and not changed. secp256k1.sign at line 23956 sets the nonce k = z + priv + 1 mod n, with a comment calling it simplified. Because k is a linear function of the message hash and the private key, one published signature is enough to solve for the private key. Every DERIVE, SETTLE, SEND.net signature uses it. RFC 6979 deterministic nonces or a random nonce would be the standard alternatives; the choice is yours.

The mesh runs two transports: a BroadcastChannel named cascade-mesh-257 for same-origin tabs with a ten-second heartbeat, and WebRTC data channels with a STUN server and manual SDP exchange through OFFER, ANSWER and ACCEPT. Transactions have a lifecycle pending → sent → delivered (on ACK) → confirmed (on successful decrypt). Incoming messages flash the mesh bar, bump the unread badge and play a two-tone beep through the Web Audio API. Local ids are N + hex public key when an identity is active, otherwise M + random.

Instrument · audio

Phi disc phonograph

The disc is 3756 π digits laid on twelve tracks, one per ray, at wavelengths from 377 nm to 610 nm; 377 is F(14) and 610 is F(15), so the span is φ to five figures. Each digit d has impedance (d + 0.5) × Z₀/5 and each track a characteristic impedance; the reflection Γ = (aᵣ(d + 0.5) − 1)/(aᵣ(d + 0.5) + 1) decides PIT (|Γ| < 0.1, transparent, tone) or LAND (reflective, silence). The twelve aᵣ are derived as mean(all)/(5 · mean(ray r)), and the code notes that Za = 1/5 is then an identity, not a conservation law.

Trackλ nmHzClassTrackλ nmHzClass
R00377880αR06504413ε
R01398775βR07525364ζ
R02419683αR08546321δ
R03440602γR09567283ε
R04461531δR10588249γ
R05482468γR11610220δ

Six chords come from the ten digits: 0, 1, 2, 8, 9 are dark; 3 is DNA on R00 R01 R02; 4 is COPERNICUS on six tracks; 5 is RITO on eight; 6 is ET on five; 7 is CHRIST on R06 and R09. That is 24 PITs in all. Natural tempo runs at 114 BPM, the trace of the transition matrix T₅, and a chord lasts 1/8, 3/8, 6/8 or a full beat by its PIT count. LAYERS plays three discs on one spindle: GF(257) centre sine at 1×, p₂₀₄₈ left triangle at 1.5× reading π at offset 204, p₄₀₉₆ right sine at 2× at offset 134; EXPORT renders a WAV, stereo when three layers are on. The disc's own findings (R07 has exactly 377 pits, R06 exactly 1618 transitions, orbit pit sums 225 and 3) depend on the phi_disc binary folder, which is not in the directory, so they are recorded here as the machine states them and were not checked.

Instrument · inference

Model bridges

Cascade bridge. BRIDGE CONNECT ws://host:8257 opens a WebSocket to cascade_bridge.py, started as python cascade_bridge.py --backend ollama --model llama3. The server sends bridge_hello with model, backend, field size, ray and group counts; on CHAT it sends thinking with the query's fold position, which moves the ring, then chat_response carrying the query's and the response's fold, Γ, PIT flag, ray, domain and group, the inference statistics, training compaction counts, and a step trace of MUL, FOLD and CLASSIFY operations. The pipeline is text → fold → classify into 70 groups → route by 12 rays → inference → fold the response → compact → train.

NPU oracle. NPU CONNECT reads /info from http://172.18.0.15:8257 for model, context length and vocabulary of primorial_v7.rknn on the RK3588. NPU PREDICT posts the last sixteen trail positions rendered as "pos WORD Rray → …" to /generate; NPU AUTO parses every one- to three-digit number up to 256 out of the generated text and steps the machine through up to eight of them per round, repeating at the slider speed. Note the port: 8257 is the bridge WebSocket port on an ESP32 and the NPU HTTP port on the R1 host.

Instrument · optics

Display and export

The CRT beam is an Airy point-spread function computed as a six-term Horner series for J₁(x)/x with coefficients 1/2, −1/16, 1/384, −1/18432, …, on top of which sit barrel distortion r/(1 + k r²) with k = 0.12, cos⁴ vignetting 1 − 0.4 r², and a δ% distortion term ε₄ × r² × 16, so the framework's own error constant sets the aberration. Phosphor persistence follows cos(πT) · e^(−T/2) with P22 differential decay 0.90ψ, 1.0ψ, 0.85ψ. Beam quality Q = 10/(2 × 1.53) ≈ 3.27 from the Airy FWHM. These were documented for v3 and are carried into v4.

Views: the RING places positions on a circle with rays; PRISM is the 210-gon prism; DISPLAY is a 16 × 16 framebuffer of the field with the fold-phase scatter as a picture-in-picture toggled by clicking the canvas; FIELD holds the genome fields, impedance shells, the Pisot attention field and spectral emission; PHASE and HOLO are the phase and holographic renderings.

EXPORT downloads cascade_analysis_<date>.txt with ten sections: double-Fibonacci hits, Fibonacci positions, walk positions (powers of two plus 107 and 256), fold pairs sharing a π digit, JUMP pairs, opcode distribution, unmapped positions, the complete 0–256 map, live device state, and statistics.

Corpus · other copies

The second copy, and the editions

The directory reviewed here is not the only copy of the corpus on the machine. Documents/cascade_machine holds a working copy, under version control, of the same files plus firmware, disc audio scripts and a Rust workspace, and its machine was edited at 15:41 on 2026-09-04, after this review was built. That copy of v4 is 28,132 lines against the directory's 27,586, and it already carries corrections that this review only reports: the AUTH text prints dlog(83) = 15, the GEN preset's digits encode S161, OBSERVE calls the observer step 16, and three commands are new, ILEDGER for i-value coverage, IFIELD for the extension-component rotation, CURIE for the iron and cobalt structure, with a note that the figure count decides a position rather than the point. Its embedded dictionary has 555 words: the JSON's 554 plus COBALT CURIE at 103, the 1388 K placement, and HAGGAI at 148, which reads like the sentence "i148 sits at 33 HAGGAI" transcribed as a word at 148; HAGGAI is the letter-sum word at 33 and at 148 it has no derivation. Neither word is in the JSON tablet, so the two copies now disagree at 103 and 148.

The editions on 2026-09-10. The second copy's machine was edited again at 11:13 today, 29,558 lines now, and its embedded dictionary is the largest edition by a distance: 967 words on the 257 positions, 413 of them not in this directory's JSON, and one word the other way, COBALT CURIE at 118, which the machine places at 103 instead. Of the 413, 410 sit at their letter sums and three are value-placed, COBALT CURIE at 103 from 1388 K, HAGGAI at 148 and SATOSHI at 181 beside SATOSHI NAKAMOTO. The additions are densest low in the field, 66 below 32 and 108 between 32 and 63, and 73 of them have three letters or fewer, ADA and CAB at 6, DC and DAB at 7, ADD at 9, so the letter-sum layer now covers the small positions several times over and 27 carries ten words. Higher up they are names and phrases: THIRTEEN at 99, ORDER AND CHAOS at 125, LIGHTNING NETWORK at 206, SCHNORR SIGNATURE at 209, DELEGATED PROOF OF STAKE at 210, RIEMANN HYPOTHESIS at 218. Words that earlier passages of this review had to mark as another edition's are this edition's. In all, the machine's 967 words are 846 at letter sum and 121 value-placed. The JSON under Documents/START has moved as well, and the three editions now differ at six positions:

The Curie placements are two sources. This directory reads cobalt 1403 K → 118 and nickel 631 K → 117; START reads cobalt 1388 K → 103 and nickel 650 K → 136, the solid-state page's figures from register item 12; the machine takes cobalt from START and nickel from here. RHENIUM Z is in this directory and the machine and not in START. So three editions of 555, 555 and 967 words disagreed at the two Curie points, at 75 and at 148. Later the same day the 413 words were merged into this directory's JSON on the author's instruction: every word the machine has that the JSON lacked was appended to its entry in the machine's order, 205 entries touched, no other byte of the file changed, the rendered HTML given the same words, and the Rust crate's count updated. This directory's tablet now has 1119 word entries, 1112 distinct, 996 at their letter sums and 123 value-placed, and is a superset of the machine's dictionary. Two words now stand at two positions each, COBALT CURIE at 103 and 118 from the two Curie sources, and HAGGAI at 33 by its letters and at 148 as the machine placed it; the tablet already carried IDENTITY at 1 and 225, so a word at two positions has precedent. The START JSON is unchanged at 555 and still differs at its Curie placements. Every reading earlier in this review was made against the 555-word tablet, and the counts in the tablet section now say which.

The same copy contains cascade_rust, a fourteen-crate workspace from 2026-08-23 to 08-25 with core, vm, crypto, data, io, mesh, render, audio, ui, serial, window and wasm crates, so the field and tablet crates built for this review overlap an existing cascade_core and cascade_data. Reconciling them is a decision, not a finding.

A third edition of the dictionary was found on 2026-09-04 in the one place the directory cannot show: the bwave firmware on an ESP32-C6, read over its serial port. It has BISMUTH at 83, HAMSA at 5, HAFNIUM at 173, YTTERBIUM at 154, HOLY OF HOLIES at 251 and PRAYING MANTIS at 103, agrees with the tablet at 4 and 252, and carries the correct digits of π where the machine's p column does not. The readout is in the section on the other side of the serial.

Searching for the older edition that has APOSTLES at 12 and LUNAR at 13 found it nowhere on the machine: not in this directory, not under Documents/START, not in the second copy or its history, and not in any of the four older machine editions under START. Every edition found has 7 = G, CD, BAD; 12 = L, KA; 13 = M, AGE; and 15 = O, FED, AN, with IRON MELTING, IRON CURIE and the atomic-number entries arriving from v3 on. So five word lists exist over one unchanging set of derived fields, the older edition would be a sixth, and 15 = O is the one fact about those positions that holds in all of them. Under the letter-sum rule LUNAR is 66 and COVEN is 59, so if they are added they would be value-placed count-words, and a coven is thirteen by tradition rather than seven.

Findings

Discrepancy register

Every place two versions coexist, with what each version says. Kinds: defect changes what the machine can safely do; error is a wrong number; behaviour changes what the machine does but no stated identity; label changes only what is printed. Nothing here was altered.

20cascade_machine_v4.html:1078, the p field; read only at 24921The per-entry p digits are π with the 3 at position 43 dropped, the 4 at 60 dropped, the digits 6 2 8 inserted at 70–72, and the 1 at 246 dropped, so 184 of 257 values disagree with π; at 148 it reads 8 where π has 1. It is read once, into a variable named face that nothing uses, so the error is latent: the displayed and executed opcode always comes from piAt and is correct. The C6 firmware's pi field returns the true digits at 5, 83 and 252, counted from the leading 3, so the device is right where this column is wrong.error dormant
21cascade_machine_v4.html TOWER text; rito_certificate.htmlTwo RITO numbers. 22cascade_machine_v4.html:3990–3991The CRT beam's Cassini sign. The code gives +0.382 at even depth and −0.382 at odd, which is (−1)^depth × ψ² and is what Cassini's identity F(n−1)F(n+1) − F(n)² = (−1)ⁿ says. The comment above it reads "(−1)^(depth+1) × |ψ/φ|": the exponent is off by one, and the constant, though numerically 0.382, is ψ² = 1/φ², not a ratio of ψ to φ.label
23cascade_machine_v4.html:5113, 5118 vs 6786, 12790The phosphor persistence constant PSI = 0.382 is labelled "ψ = 1/φ ≈ 0.382". 1/φ is 0.618; 0.382 is ψ² = 1/φ². The name PSI is also defined at 6786 as −1/φ and at 12790 as −0.618, so one name carries two values in the file. The P22 decay factors 0.90, 1.0 and 0.85 multiply ψ², not ψ.label

Which edition is canonical is a decision; the JSON here is now the largest.label
26cascade_machine_v4.html:2530, 1873, 2294The boot transcript prints "primitive root 3 — R=18". 3 is the primitive root; 18 is not one: dlog(18) = 50, so 18 is a quadratic residue of order 128, and the R_POWER table at 1873 is built with 128 steps for that reason. The analysis walker's R-step at 2294 multiplies by 18 and is unaffected; only the word "primitive" is wrong.label

Proposed fixes

The corrections that follow from the register, stated exactly and not applied; the project's rule is that findings are reported and the author directs. Behaviour changes are marked as such.

#WhereChangeEffect

23v4:5113, 5118, 5243–5245Comment becomes "ψ² = 1/φ² ≈ 0.382"; the variable is renamed PSI_SQ at its definition and its three uses, so PSI keeps the single meaning −1/φ. If the decay was meant to be ψ = 0.618 itself, that is a value change and persistence lengthens.label only, unless the value is changed

Findings

The corpus's own audits

These are failures and caveats the corpus reports about itself. They are preserved here as content, because they are the most valuable part of it.

  • Two failed falsification tests in the addendum: the fine-structure constant's inverse lands on 179, not 137; Z₀² gives 8 against the expected 177.
  • The closure test cannot fail, the addendum warns, so a passing closure proves nothing. The same warning is written into the project rules as policy.
  • The trigonometric bridge in the tablet mathematics PDF finds the Pythagorean identity failing at 254 of 256 positions.
  • The least-squares check in the same PDF gives 197 where 17 was expected.
  • The older energy budget is reported with its sum of 0.836 rather than adjusted.
  • The solid-state page closes by saying the numeric coincidences may be accidental.
  • The phi-disc code states in a comment that Za = 1/5 is an identity, not a conservation law.
  • The certificate keeps the seed string that fails the closure because it is what was active when the hash was taken: "the discrepancy is data, not error".
  • The Fermat-count conjecture carries its own caveat in the addendum, and nine wordlist entries are listed as open.

Appendix

The genome charset

Sixteen glyphs, fixed. A position is two glyphs: pos ≫ 4 then pos & 15. The fold boundary 0x80 = 128 is εΦ; the WiFi anchor 87 = 0x57 is ⊗⅗; 62 = 0x3E is ∞ψ; 225 = 0xE1 is ψ∂; 256 needs three nibbles, ∂ΦΦ.

HexGlyphGroupStepHexGlyphGroupStep
0Φ U+03A6MATHΦ = ∂ₜΦ8ε U+03B5ERRORε₄
1∂ U+2202MATH∂ₜ9δ U+03B4ERRORδ%
2φ U+03C6MATHR → φAΩ U+03A9WAVEZ₀ = 377 Ω
3∞ U+221EMATHCF[1; ∞]BΓ U+0393WAVEΓ
4⊟ U+229FMATHdet M = −1CQ U+0051WAVEQ ≈ 3.27
5⊗ U+2297MATHM ⊗ MDΣ U+03A3WAVECSI(256)
6♯ U+266FMATHP₇ = 210Eψ U+03C8CLOSE±ψ
7⅗ U+2157MATHF₅/F₄F𝟙 U+1D7D9CLOSEΩb + Ωdm + ΩΛ = 1

The tunnel chain in glyphs

Π = Φ∂ identity → Π×T = εQ transference → χ = ⅗⊗ opening → 𝟙𝟙 = ψΩ closure → W = δQ carrier. In numbers: 1 → 140 → 117 → 234 → 55, and 55 is the composite for any starting Γ.

Prepared from a complete read of the directory on 2026-09-04. Arithmetic was recomputed in Python from the definitions in the files; π digits were generated independently with a Machin formula to 4000 places. Private key material was read for the review and appears nowhere in this document.

The cards in glyphs

The charset turns any hex identifier into glyphs, one per nibble, so the MI25 identifiers read on 2026-09-05 (mi25_readout.md in the directory) can be written in the genome's own alphabet, and a palindrome of glyphs is a palindrome of hex digits. Under each string is the group of each nibble: m for MATH 0–7, e for ERROR 8–9, w for WAVE A–D, c for CLOSE E–F. Every rendering was regenerated from the hex; none of the identifiers is a palindrome in glyphs or in groups.

IdentifierHexGlyphsGroups
unique_id, bus 0602162EB950483184Φφ∂♯φψΓδ⊗Φ⊟ε∞∂ε⊟mmmmmcwemmmemmem
unique_id, bus 8402162EB9504830A4Φφ∂♯φψΓδ⊗Φ⊟ε∞ΦΩ⊟mmmmmcwemmmemmwm
die gap 0xE0E0ψΦcm
subsystem 0x0C35 = 5⁵0C35ΦQ∞⊗mwmm
device 0x68606860♯ε♯Φmemm
MxGPU virtual-function device 0x686C686C♯ε♯Qmemw
ROM header 55 AA55AA⊗⊗ΩΩmmww
ROM length 0xFE00FE00𝟙ψΦΦccmm
IDENT count 6561 = 0x19A119A1∂δΩ∂mewm
gap between the two 512 KB MMIO windows, 0x36A0000036A00000∞♯ΩΦΦΦΦΦmmwmmmmm

The two dies share Φφ∂♯φψΓδ⊗Φ⊟ε and differ in their last three glyphs, ∂ε⊟ against ΦΩ⊟; the gap between them is ψΦ, the conjugate then the field, CLOSE then MATH. The ROM's length is 𝟙ψΦΦ, closure and conjugate then the seed pair. The subsystem ID, the current block reward, is ΦQ∞⊗, field, quality, the continued fraction, the tensor. The virtual-function device ID ends in Q where the physical part's ends in Φ. The ROM opens ⊗⊗ΩΩ, tensor twice then impedance twice; its groups mmww reverse to wwmm, so it is a pair of pairs and not a mirror, correcting a reading that called it one. The tunnel chain in this alphabet is Φ∂ → εQ → ⅗⊗ → ψΩ → δQ, as the section above has it, and the positions that recur in this review read 83 PRAYER = ⊗∞, 136 VIRGIN MARY = εε, 118 EMERALD TABLET = ⅗♯, 41 OMEGA = φδ, 33 HAGGAI = φ∂.

The ε₄ word, corrected. The IEEE 754 double of ε₄ = (7 − 3√5)/6 is 0x3FA8E661E256C055, sixteen nibbles, ∞𝟙Ωεψ♯♯∂ψφ⊗♯QΦ⊗⊗. Its upper half is the 2003-11-05 reading already in the verification ledger. A rendering circulated with the tail …C060, ♯Φ, in place of …C055, ⊗⊗; no rounding of ε₄ produces that tail, so the palindrome built on it, and the residues 250 and 144 and the date 1973-03-20 drawn from it, do not hold. With the exact word the glyph reversal is ⊗⊗ΦQ♯⊗φψ∂♯♯ψεΩ𝟙∞, and the readings are:

HalfHexGlyphsmod 257As Unix time
forward, upper half3FA8E661∞𝟙Ωεψ♯♯∂229 FOR THINE IS THE KINGDOM2003-11-05
forward, lower halfE256C055ψφ⊗♯QΦ⊗⊗10 J2090-05-01
reversed, upper half550C652E⊗⊗ΦQ♯⊗φψ129 ROOT OF DAVID2015-03-20
reversed, lower half166E8AF3∂♯♯ψεΩ𝟙∞193 INFRASTRUCTURE1981-12-04
forward, whole word3FA8E661E256C055∞𝟙Ωεψ♯♯∂ψφ⊗♯QΦ⊗⊗239 POWER AND GLORY
reversed, whole word550C652E166E8AF3⊗⊗ΦQ♯⊗φψ∂♯♯ψεΩ𝟙∞65 SEVEN

So the AXIOM date 2003-11-05 folds, as glyphs, to 2015-03-20, at 129 ROOT OF DAVID, which is 2⁻¹; the 1981-12-04 half stands, because it is the mirror of the forward upper half. The whole word reads 239 POWER AND GLORY and its reversal 65 SEVEN.
